2. RECOGNITION 
 
B. Mission Moment – TSD Preschool Artwork 
 
As this evening’s Mission Moment, we would like to take the opportunity to recognize 
3rd Grade Artists at Leonard Elementary School who connected their learning to the 
world, by participating in a district-wide art contest to create images for the new TSD 
Preschool Building.  During Art Class, students were challenged to create a unique 
water or land scene while also demonstrating their ability to mix the tints and shades 
of a color.  The top four works from the school were submitted to the contest, and all 
four were selected as winning entries! 
 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Invite Amy Mee and Erin Keyser up to  
     introduce the winning young artists. 
 
 
Leonard 3rd Graders 
Sophia Mills 
Deepak Sabbireddy 
Jordan Smith 
Lillie Dupuis

                                  2. C.     02.26.19 
 
 
2.       RECOGNITION 
 
C.  Principal Appreciation Month 
Throughout the State of Michigan, February has traditionally been celebrated as 
Principal Appreciation Month, in recognition of the outstanding work of our 
building-level educational leaders. In Troy, we are fortunate to have an exemplary 
group of principals dedicated to the success of every student in the District. This 
evening, we will pass a resolution to commend our elementary and secondary 
principals for their exceptional commitment to excellence in the Troy School 
District.  
 
WHEREAS, energetic and inspiring school leadership is essential if teachers, 
schools and districts are to ensure that all Troy students are college  and career 
ready; and, 
  
WHEREAS, K-12 school principals play a vital role in the success of students and 
act as the liaison between the school and the community it serves, ensuring that 
parents and the community are aware of student and school achievements; and, 
  
WHEREAS, Troy School District principals are dedicated,  quality educational 
professionals committed to providing sound leadership and guidance to their staff 
and students; and,  
  
WHEREAS, during this month, we join with educators, parents and students 
throughout Michigan to raise awareness of the importance of educational 
leadership, and to recognize and thank all principals working in Troy for their 
exemplary service; 
  
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED , that the Troy Board of Education 
officially recognizes February as Principal Appreciation Month in Troy and thanks 
our dedicated building leaders for all they do for our students and staff.    
 
 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Read and adopt resolution.

  Supported by:

Dr.  Philippart,  and  Dr.  Wilson.  Mrs.  Fleming  was  absent.  Also  p resent  were 
Superintendent  Machesky,  administrators  Carson,  Griffor,  Dziatczak,  Harris,  Moynihan, 
and West.  There was one member in the audience.

Establish Fund Depositories and 
Investments  
19‐007 
 
 
It was moved by Mrs. Hammond and supported by Mr. 
Hauff that BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED  that the Board 
of Education adopts the resolution on Fund Depositories 
and  Investments  for  the  period  January  through 
December  2019,  and  that  a  copy  of  the  attached 
resolution, in its entirety, be incorporated as part of the 
official minutes of the Board of Education. 
 
WHEREAS,  there  may  be  in  and  may,  hereafter  from 
time  to  time,  come  into  the  hands  of  Rick  West, 
Treasurer of Troy School District, certain public monies 
belonging  to  or  held  for  the  State,  County,  or  other 
political units of the State or otherwise held according to 
law, and 
 
WHEREAS,  under  the  laws  of  Michigan,  this  Board  is 
required to provide by Resolution for the deposit of all 
public  monies,  including  tax  monies  coming  into  the 
hands of said Treasurer, on one or more banks, hereafter 
called bank(s) to be designated in such Resolution. 
 
NOW THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED  that said Treasurer, 
Rick West, is hereby directed to deposit and/or invest all 
public  monies,  including  tax  monies  now  in  or  coming 
into his hands as Treasurer in his name as Treasurer, into 
the following depository institutions: 
 
Bank of America 
Bank of New York 
Charter One Bank 
JP Morgan Chase Bank 
Comerica Bank 
Huntington Bank 
Fifth Third Bank 
Flagstar Bancorp 
MILAF (Michigan Liquid Asset Fund Plus, managed 
By PFM Asset Management, Inc. through Fifth Third 
Bank) 
11
  5 January 15, 2019 
 
 
Morgan Stanley Smith Barney 
PFM Securities, Inc. through Fifth Third Bank 
PNC Bank

RECOGNITION 
TSD Mission Moment – Baker 
Middle School Food Drive 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Board Recognition Month 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
A s  t h i s  e v e n i n g ’ s  M i s s i o n  M o m e n t ,  t h e  B o a r d  
recognized  Baker  Middle  School  for  their  efforts  in 
hosting their second annual food drive.  The Food Drive 
was facilitated in Social Studies classrooms and run by 
a  student  force  of  approximately  50  students.  All  the 
f o o d  c o l l e c t e d  w e n t  t o  r o u g h l y  1 0 0  T r o y  E l e m e n t a r y  
and  Middle  School  students  who  receive  free  and 
reduced  lunches.  Due  to  the  large  quantity  collected 
this year, donations will be able to provide support for 
Baker  and  Morse  families  for  several  months!    The 
Board asked Jonathan Cross, principal of Baker Middle 
School, to come up and say a few words about the food 
d r i v e .   M r .  C r o s s  s h a r e d  a  b r i e f  v i d e o  w i t h  t h e  B o a r d  
and  introduced  the  staff  and  students  who  organized 
the drive.  Staff and students received congratulations 
from the Board along with a certificate of recognition.
 
 
 
 
Each  January,  the  Troy  School  District  recognizes  and 
celebrates  the  work  of  a  very  important  group  of 
individuals: The Board of Education.  
 
Our trustees work very hard to make the Troy School 
District a World Class district and provide students with 
amazing opportunities to learn and grow. This year, the 
Board will see the realization of a project that will offer 
even more children in Troy the opportunity for a high‐
quality  education  when  we  open  our  Troy  School 
District Preschool this fall.  
 
This evening, as a token of the District’s appreciation for 
the  Board’s  tireless  efforts  on  behalf  of  our  students 
and  staff,  we  present  you  with  four  tile  coasters—
featuring  elementary  student  art  that  will  adorn  the 
14
  8 January 15, 2019 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
walls  of  our  new  preschool  facility.  These  tiles 
r e p r e s e n t  n a t u r e ,  f r o m  o u r  f o r e s t s  t o  o u r  w a t e r ,  a n d  
symbolize  the  connection  with  our  natural 
surroundings and the world around us that we intend 
t o  b e  a  f o c u s  o f  o u r  p r e s c h o o l  p r o g r a m s .  T h r o u g h  
careful planning and the support of this Board, we know 
that our new Troy School District Preschool will truly be 
a  place  where  exploration  begins  for  our  youngest 
learners.    We  hope  that  this  small  gift  will  serve  as  a 
reminder of our gratitude to you,  our Board trustees, 
for your support that will allow our preschool students 
to Explore, Create and Connect in a World Class setting. 
Thank you for all you do.

Board Committee Assignments 
19‐019 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
FIRST READING: Revisions to Board 
Policies 
 
 
 
 
 
I t  w a s  m o v e d  b y  M r s .  F l e m i n g  a n d  s u p p o r t e d  b y  M r .  
Gottlieb that BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED  that the Troy 
Board of Education hereby adopts the Board Committee 
Memberships for the 2019 school year as attached. 
 
Yes:  7 
No:  0 
 
 
 
 
Members of the Board Policy Committee have reviewed 
the Board Policies listed below. All of these policies have 
been reviewed by the Board of Education’s attorney to 
ensure they comply with State law, the Open Meetings 
Act, and the Revised School Code.  The first reading of 
18
  12 January 15, 2019 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Board Comments 
these  revised  and  or  additional  Board  Policies  are 
presented at this evening’s meeting. 
0000.02  The  Board  of  Education  (Revision),  2000.03 
Education  Records  (Addition),  2000.07 H e a l t h  &  
Safety/Cardiac Emergency Response Plan (Addition from 
8000.05),  4000.03  Conditions  of  Employment/Criminal 
Background Checks (Revision), 4000.05 Other Matters of 
Employment/Prohibition  of  Referral  or  Assistance 
(Addition),  5000.10 M e a l  C h a r g e / F o o d  S e r v i c e s  
(Addition),  6000.05  Tobacco‐Free  Environment/ 
Electronic  Cigarettes,  Vaporizers,  Etc.  (Revision  Add 
language  from  6000.05), 6000.06  Electronic  Cigarettes 
(Deletion  Combine  with  6000.05),  7000.07 
Advertising/District  Support  Organization  (Addition), 
8000.05 C a r d i a c  E m e r g e n c y  R e s p o n s e  P l a n  ( D e l e t i o n  
Move to 2000.07); and 8000.10 Digital Communications 
(Addition). 
 
All Board members were asked to review these policies 
for a second time prior to the next Board meeting and 
will vote on adoption of such under the consent agenda 
at the February 26
th Board meeting. 
 
 
Dr.  Philippart  welcomed  Dr.  Wilson  to  her  first  Board 
Meeting.  
 
Mrs.  Fleming  welcomed  Dr.  Wilson  and  congratulated 
her,  Mr.  Schmidt  and  Mr.  Hauff  for  swearing  in  for 
another term as Board members. 
 
Mr.  Hauff  thanked  Mr.  Cichowicz  and  Schroeder  5
th 
grade students for inviting him to attend their cow heart 
dissection event and thanked the TFEE for funding the 
project.  He shared how impressed he was with the focus 
and professionalism of the students. 
 
Mr. Gottlieb also welcomed Dr. Wilson to the Board. 
 
M r .  S c h m i d t  g a v e  a  s h o u t  o u t  t o  R y a n  I r l a ,  W o r l d  
Geography  teacher  at  Athens  for  taking  a  risk  on  a 
globalization  project  with  his  students  asking  them  to 
create a video showing evidence of globalization in Troy.  
This was a true example of students connecting learning 
to their world.   
 
Dr.  Machesky  announced  that  the  new  Troy  School 
District  Preschool  is  holding  their  Informational  Night 
this evening at Troy High School.  He shared that it was 
a full house with nearly 140 families already registered.   
19
  13 January 15, 2019 
 
 
He  also  shared  that  the  annual  Martin  Luther  King,  Jr. 
Day  of  Service  will  be  held  at  Athens  High  School  on 
Monday, January 21
st.  The District is holding this event 
in partnership with the City of Troy and encouraged the 
community  to  come  out  and  do  something  that  has 
meaningful impact in our community. 
 
Mr.  Schmidt  shared  that  the  next  Board  Workshop 
would be held on Tuesday, February 5
th at 6:00 p.m. in 
the Administration Building and the next regular Board 
of Education Meeting will be held on Tuesday, February 
26
th at 7:00 p.m. in the Services Building.  He explained 
that the meeting is a week later due to mid‐winter break.  
 
 
ADJOURNMENT  The meeting was adjourned at 8:15 p.m. 
 
Respectfully submitted, 
 
 
 
Gary N. Hauff 
Secretary, Troy Board of Education

 7. B.   02.26.19 
 
7. CURRICULUM 
 
B.  CONSIDERATION:   Schools Closed to Open Enrollment for  
                                        2019-2020 
 
Administration has reviewed the enrollment for elementary, middle, and high 
school attendance areas.  Due to the elementary and middle school  buildings’ 
total student enrollment and physical space constraints, we recommend that 
Barnard, B emis, Schroeder and Boulan Park  Schools be closed to open 
enrollment.  Our goal  for Athens and Troy High  has been to maximize each 
building’s enrollment which has resulted in  both schools being closed to open 
enrollment for the past several years. Based on current enrollment projections 
for the 2019-2020 school year, it is recommended that Athens and Troy High be 
closed to open enrollment with the exception of not more than (25) twenty-five 
incoming ninth grade students who reside in the Troy High School attendance 
area but wish to attend Athens High School.  Applications for the (25) twenty-
five spots at Athens High School will be accepted during a two-week window at 
the beginning of March. 
 
 
 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Adopt the following resolution. 
 
 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 
 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
 
 
Be it therefore resolved that the Troy Board of Education adopts a resolution to close 
Barnard, Bemis, Schroeder and Boulan Park Schools as well as Athens and Troy High 
Schools to open enrollment for the 2019-2020 school year, with the exception of not 
more than (25) twenty-five incoming ninth grade students who reside in the Troy High 
School attendance area but wish to attend Athens High School.  Students with siblings 
attending Athens and Troy High and currently enrolled in grades 9 – 11 will be allowed 
to open enroll to remain for the 2019-2020 school year.  
 
Yes: __________ 
 
No: __________

7. CURRICULUM 
 
D. CONSIDERATION:   Schools of Choice Section 105 & 105c  
 
In accordance with Michigan state law, school districts shall pass a resolution 
each school year if they intend to participate in a Schools of Choice program.  
It is anticipated that a limited number of seats will be available for the 2019-
2020 school year for kindergarten, first grade, and grades 6th - 8th at Baker, 
Larson and Smith  Middle Schools.  It would be appropriate at this time to 
consider adopting a resolution for Schools of Choice Section 105 and 105c for 
(50) fifty kindergarten, (1) one first grade, and (20) twenty total 6th – 8th grade 
nonresident students who attend a school district within or outside of  Oakland 
County, if that intermediate school district is contiguous with the Oakland 
Intermediate School District boundaries.   
  
 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 
 Moved by: __________________________ 
 
 Supported by: __________________________ 
 
 
Be it therefore resolved that the Board of Education of the Troy School 
District, exercising the option permitted by Section 105 & 105c of the State 
School Aid Act of 1979, as amended by Public Act 300 of 1996, will accept 
applications for a Limited Schools of Choice program for the 2019-2020 school 
year for (50) fifty kindergarten, (1) one first grade, and (20) twenty total 6 th 
– 8th grade nonresident students who attend a school district within or outside 
of  Oakland County, if that intermediate school district is contiguous with the 
Oakland Intermediate School District boundaries.

No: __________

the addendum came out to late to get an accurate price so we added 
a little to the 10' quote that we had before the memo. In addition 
because no detail was provided in regards to the attachment of the 
bridge we will have to negotiate that out after a final design is 
completed. 
134
*Due to the custom nature of the product, all dimensions are approximate. 
Actual sizes may differ slightly.
Cement Plaster Specifications
Coloring Agents
All rockwork and colored paving construction shall be 
colored integrally and/or colored with dust on color 
hardeners or acid stain surface coloring or concrete 
water based stain.
1. Integral Color: Shall be “Chromix” , as manufactured 
by L.M. Scofield Co; Los Angeles, CA or equal.
2. Dust: on color hardeners shall be “Lithochrome” 
Color Hardener by L.M. Scofield Co. Los Angeles, CA 
or equal.
3. Acid Stain: Shall be “Lithochrome” acid base stains as 
manufactured by L.M. Scofield Co.: Los Angeles, CA
4. Modified exterior latex paints: As needed for final 
color highlighting equal to Sherwin Williams outdoor 
concrete stain.
5. All other special features: Shall be colored with owner 
approved materials submitted by specialty Contractor.
E. Miscellaneous Special Materials: All features created 
by Specialty Contractor shall have drawings indicating 
materials to be used and shall be submitted and 
approved by owner prior to construction.
ThemeCrete® Specifications
Embossing Mortar and Cement Plaster is site mixed 
cement/aggregate meeting the same criteria as GFRC, 
but omitting the glass fibers; for filling the irregular 
voids between mold cast panels and/or special themed 
features. 
Themecrete® formula is a mixture with a Glass Fiber 
Reinforced Concrete (GFRC) base. GFRC is a cement/
aggregate slurry reinforced throughout with chopped 
glass fibers. Cement used is Type 1 Portland cement 
meeting the requirement of the ASTM C 150. Sand 
used is washed silica sand passing a U.S. No.20 sieve, 
and conforming to ASTM C 144. Glass Fibers used are 
alkali-resistant (AR) fiber specifically designed for use 
in concrete and chopped to 1”-2” lengths. Thickness 
of the cast and built up surfaces and panels shall be 
a minimum of 5/8” and up, depending on the face 
configuration. 
Backup Material
Shall be galvanized steel “diamond lathe” , tied in place 
with annealed steel wire and reinforced with #3 or #4 
steel reinforcing rods. Minimum physical properties 
shall be: 1500 pounds per square inch for tension and 
5000 pounds per square inch for compression.

glass fibers. Cement used is Type 1 Portland cement 
meeting the requirement of the ASTM C 150. Sand 
used is washed silica sand passing a U.S. No.20 sieve, 
and conforming to ASTM C 144. Glass Fibers used are 
alkali-resistant (AR) fiber specifically designed for use 
in concrete and chopped to 1”-2” lengths. Thickness 
of the cast and built up surfaces and panels shall be 
a minimum of 5/8” and up, depending on the face 
configuration. 
Backup Material
Shall be galvanized steel “diamond lathe” , tied in place 
with annealed steel wire and reinforced with #3 or #4 
steel reinforcing rods. Minimum physical properties 
shall be: 1500 pounds per square inch for tension and 
5000 pounds per square inch for compression.

Learning Department as part of the Troy School District Preschool/ECC Project.   
 
Keri Seeney of TMP Architecture led a project team with Kris Griffor and Erin Keyser to develop 
a furniture package to outfit the new Troy School District Preschool Building.  The project team

contracts (except for Category Q, which was solicited for 3 quotes and the cost is below the

categories, price amounts & cooperative bid contracts they were sourced from.

will be from the ECC Project Fund.

2 Candidates for MASB Board of Directors
MASB Bylaws – Article IV – Membership
Section 2. Active Members. The board of education of any public school district in Michigan may 
become an active member of the Association. Active members of the Association shall be divided 
into eight regions. The regions shall be designated according to intermediate school district 
boundaries. The regions shall be as follows: 
Region 1. Copper Country ISD, Delta-Schoolcraft ISD, Dickinson-Iron ISD, Eastern Upper 
Peninsula ISD, Gogebic-Ontonagon ISD, Marquette-Alger RESA and Menominee County ISD. 
Region 2. Alpena-Montmorency-Alcona ESD, Charlevoix- Emmet ISD, Cheboygan-Otsego-Presque 
Isle ESD, COOR ISD, Iosco RESA, Manistee ISD, Traverse Bay Area ISD and Wexford-Missaukee 
ISD. 
Region 3. Ionia County ISD, Kent ISD, Mecosta-Osceola ISD, Montcalm Area ISD, Muskegon 
Area ISD, Newaygo County RESA, Ottawa Area ISD and West Shore ESD. 
Region 4. Bay-Arenac ISD, Clare-Gladwin RESD, Clinton County RESA, Gratiot-Isabella RESD, 
Midland County ESA, Saginaw ISD and Shiawassee RESD. 
Region 5. Genesee ISD, Huron ISD, Lapeer County ISD, Sanilac ISD, St. Clair County RESA and 
Tuscola ISD. 
Region 6. Allegan Area ESA, Barry ISD, Berrien RESA, Branch ISD, Calhoun ISD, Kalamazoo 
RESA, Lewis Cass ISD, St. Joseph County ISD and Van Buren ISD. 
Region 7. Eaton RESA, Hillsdale County ISD, Ingham ISD, Jackson County ISD, Lenawee ISD, 
Livingston ESA, Monroe County ISD and Washtenaw ISD. 
Region 8. Macomb ISD, Oakland Schools and Wayne RESA. 
Active members shall also be divided into the following seven groups based on pupil membership. 
Group I  All intermediate districts; 
Group II  School districts with a pupil membership of 0 - 1,400; 
Group III  School districts with a pupil membership of 1,401 - 2,500; 
Group IV  School districts with a pupil membership of 2,501 - 5,000; 
Group V  School districts with a pupil membership of 5,001 - 11,000; 
Group VI  School districts with a pupil membership of 11,001 - 40,000; and
Group VII  School districts with a pupil membership over 40,000.

4 Candidates for MASB Board of Directors
Board of Directors’ Elections—Candidate Information  
and Email Voting Procedures
This year, seven seats on the MASB Board of Directors are up for election. 
The MASB Board of Directors is comprised of 19 members—16 directors elected from the eight regions (two 
elected from each); and one director each from Groups V, VI and VII. Directors are elected by active member 
school boards and, each year, several seats are up for election. As approved by the 2018 Delegate Assembly, 
beginning in 2019 and in each subsequent year, the MASB President will nominate a member to serve as an at-
large director, subject to Board approval. Beginning in 2021, the Board of Directors will consist of 22 members, 
including three at-large directors. 
Board Limitations:
Article VIII, Section 6 of the MASB Bylaws permits only one director to be elected or appointed from any one 
local or intermediate school district board. 
According to MASB Bylaws Article VIII, Section 8, if any nomination for the Board of Directors is unopposed, 
the Board of Directors shall declare the unopposed candidate elected without conducting an election for the 
uncontested group or region.
This year, the following seats on the MASB Board are up for election (one seat each, three-year terms unless 
otherwise noted): Regions 3, 4, 6, (two-year term) 7 and 8, and Groups V and VI; if you’re unsure of your 
district’s region or group, please see the list on page 2.
• Region 4 will continue to be represented by Ruth Coppens, Saginaw ISD (unopposed, no ballots will    
be sent)
• Group VI will continue to be represented by Shawna Walker, Wayne-Westland Community Schools 
(unopposed, no ballots will be sent)
Voting in Regions 3, 6, 7 and 8 and Group V will take place via an online ballot. No local district/ISD funds 
can be used to campaign for anyone running for a seat. To help your board make an informed decision, the 
following pages contain background information and statements from each of the candidates.
Each district received ONE ballot, which was emailed to the superintendent secretary on record with MASB on 
Jan. 24, 2019. These ballots CANNOT be forwarded to anyone else. Once your board makes a decision on 
which candidate it wishes to vote for, please convey this information to your superintendent secretary.
Completed ballots must be submitted and confirmed by the superintendent secretary by 1 p.m. on Wednesday, 
March 6, 2019.
Electronic voting is a two-step process:
 Step 1 = Submit ballot.
 Step 2 = Confirm ballot has been submitted.
Unofficial election results will be available on the voicemail recording at 517.327.5915 as soon as possible, but 
generally after 2 p.m. on the deadline date. Official results will be approved at the March 22, 2019 Board of 
Directors’ meeting, which newly elected directors should plan to attend.
To summarize:
 • Ballot deadline is 1 p.m. on Wednesday, March 6, 2019.
 • No local district/ISD funds can be used to campaign for anyone running for a seat.
 • Have questions? Contact Cheryl Huffman at 517.327.5915 or chuffman@masb.org.

2019 Elections 5
Region 3 (Three-Year Term)
Kandace Boysen Mona Shores Public Schools, Muskegon County
Time served on this board: 11 years
Offices held: President and Vice President
MASB Certification:
 Certified Boardmember Award
 Award of Merit
 Award of Distinction
 Master Boardmember Award
 Master Diamond Award
Election Statement:
I have always appreciated the public education our two sons had received throughout their K-12 years. When 
a seat was available for appointment in July 2007, I applied because I wanted to be a part of making sure that 
each and every student would have all possible opportunities to succeed. During my time as a Mona Shores 
Public Schools board trustee, I have become greatly invested in PBIS and the successes this program provides 
for students. I advocate for each student having his/her own learning style and that the appropriate education 
is available and is professionally provided. I encourage and urge our state legislators to make the investment 
for each student and to have the professionals and the tools present daily for the success of each student. 
Considering the current state and federal legislative climates for public education, I am concerned the separation 
of church and state will not be upheld unless we advocates are tenacious at keeping our elected officials in 
check. As a locally elected public servant, I have a passion for maintaining the autonomy of publicly elected 
boards of education. I hope I have the opportunity to demonstrate how deeply I feel about public education to 
our fellow MASB members. 
I am a retired Certified Registered Nurse Anesthetist. I began my nursing career in 1973. Within a few years of 
practicing I realized I wanted to be a nurse specialist and chose nurse anesthesia. I have worked in Pittsburgh 
and Baltimore until my husband and I moved to Muskegon in 1986. I continued working as a CRNA until I 
retired in 1994 to be a stay-at-home mom. 
I have often commented that I am amazed at the work our educators do and that I could never fill any of their 
shoes. I look forward to the day when all teachers are appreciated and recognized widely for the levels of 
education they have attained and for how they continually utilize their skills and professionalism to set the stage 
for their students’ successes.

2019 Elections 7
Region 3 (Three-Year Term)
John Siemion Grand Haven Area Public Schools, Ottawa County
Time served on this board: 20 years
Offices held: President, Vice President and Treasurer
Time served on another board: Two years, Ottawa Area ISD
Offices held: Secretary
MASB Certification:
 Certified Boardmember Award
 Award of Merit
 Award of Distinction
 Master Boardmember Award
 Master Diamond Award
    Master Platinum Award
    President’s Award
Election Statement:
I have had the pleasure of serving on the Grand Haven Area Public Schools Board of Education since 1999. 
For the past five years, I have served as President. I have also been a member of the Ottawa Area ISD Board of 
Education since July 2017 and have served on the MASB Government Relations Committee. 
My campaign promise was to become the best board member I could be, and the best way to achieve that, for 
me, was to become certified through MASB’s CBA program. I have gained a wealth of knowledge by attending 
classes and networking with board members from around the state. 
Public education is as diverse as our country; there is no single fix-all solution that works for all schools. I strongly 
believe that those closest to the problems facing public education should be the ones making the decisions 
to improve our schools. Receiving mandates by state and federal legislators far removed from the day-to-day 
operations of local districts are seldom effective in raising student achievement. 
Advocating for our state to provide adequate and equitable funding for ALL districts continues to be a goal of 
mine. Adequate funding is sufficient funding to provide basic schooling. Equitable funding is based on fairness. 
My goal is to work with local legislators to provide equitable funding for all schools based on their needs, 
moving away from “one size fits all” funding. 
All boards face the occasional tough decision. Whenever I am faced with making a difficult decision regarding 
our students’ education, I first obtain the data and conduct background research to back up my decision, and 
then I vote for what I believe is best for our children. One of my proudest moments serving my local district 
occurred while conducting a superintendent search in 2015. Through my leadership, our board placed a heavy 
emphasis on the qualities our staff, students, parents, business partners and community members wanted to 
see in a new superintendent. After a lengthy process, I believe we hired the best of the best—and our district’s 
accomplishments in recent years support our decision. If elected as a member of the MASB Board of Directors, 
I would apply this same methodology and leadership style to each situation we face. It would be an honor to 
serve.

Election Statement:
My first exposure to MASB was within maybe a week of my assuming office on the Board of Education. Mary 
Kerwin came out and taught us about the role of boards and board members. From this first interaction, I could 
tell MASB meant business and brought immense value to its member districts. Since then, I’ve attended almost 
every conference and class reasonably available to me.
In only two years on the Board, I’ve already reached the Award of Merit and Advocacy Skills certifications. 
I worked on the task force convened by Don Wotruba to evaluate and improve the equitability of MASB’s 
governance model. I am constantly reading DashBoard articles to keep up on trends in education and utilizing 
“News From the Capitol” to advocate for public education with my elected officials. At the 2018 Annual 
Conference, I coordinated and facilitated a panel session of Young Leaders to provide our perspective to fellow 
school board members, and I soon hope to establish a Young Leaders Caucus within MASB.
Each of these points demonstrates that I am all-in with MASB, and I am seeking to take that commitment to the 
next level and serve each of you as a member of the Board of Directors.
Research tells us that more diverse teams achieve better outcomes and highlights the importance of having 
leaders in education who look and sound like the diverse populations we serve. The unique quality I bring to the 
Board of Directors is my perspectives as a young leader. I know what it’s like for students as digital natives, the 
pressures and restraints of standardized testing, and what the modern classroom looks and feels like from the 
students’ viewpoint. Just as I have brought this to my district, this point of view puts me in a unique position to 
bring that perspective to your Board of Directors and help inform the decisions and discussions that occur there.
I am a team-oriented leader. You’ll notice these paragraphs don’t contain a list of objectives or platforms. That’s 
because a board is not the place for individual agendas. A board is a place for teamwork, collaboration, 
deliberation and advancement of the collective good of an organization. In bringing my perspective to the Board 
of Directors, I commit that the teamwork, collaboration, and decisions made will be more robust and MASB will 
be a better organization as a result of my involvement and leadership.

Election Statement:
Everything we do as board members is in service to an equitable and exceptional public education for all 
students. I believe that ensuring learning for all by providing students with authentic, personally meaningful, deep 
learning experiences is a powerful way to tackle inequity. Ensuring learning is an adaptive challenge. There isn’t 
a ready-made solution that fits all students. For this reason, it is critical that our local boards are fully supported 
as they manage this complex and ambiguous task of providing deep learning experiences for each student. Local 
control is essential for making nimble decisions that meet the needs of students within each community across our 
state.
I have served the Troy community as a board member for the past two years. Before that, I taught in Troy for 
41 years—mostly at the high school level. I have taught vocational auto mechanics, photography, computer 
networks and repair, government and economics—to name a few. While teaching in Troy, I was engaged with 
the Troy Education Association as Building Rep and as Grievance Chair. I was also Chair of the South Oakland 
District Association Council.
In addition to my experiences in education, I have served on the Troy City Council and the Troy Planning 
Commission. I have owned and operated a real estate business in Troy for more than 35 years. I am currently 
serving on the Board of Directors of the T & I Credit Union in Clawson and the Clawson Chamber of Commerce.
MASB aims to provide high quality educational leadership services for all Michigan boards of education. 
The multiple perspectives (educator, teacher advocate, board member) that I can bring to the MASB Board of 
Directors will provide a unique lens for this work. I have a deep understanding of quality professional learning 
that will assist MASB in building the capacity of board members to ensure equity through excellence for all 
students.

Group V (Three-Year Term)
Lisa M. Kreager Rockford Public Schools, Kent County
Time served on this board: Six years
Offices held: President, Vice President, Secretary and Treasurer
MASB Certification:
 Certified Boardmember Award
 Award of Merit
  
  
Election Statement:
As a two-term elected School Board Trustee for Rockford Public Schools, one of my greatest concerns regarding 
our public schools is equitable funding. As a district with base foundation funding, I know firsthand, the 
economic challenges we face with the rising costs of retirement funding and health care costs, unfunded 
mandates, and keeping our students and staff safe in our buildings. How does educating a child in one district 
warrant only half the funding of a child in a neighboring district? 
Next, I would address the need to bring mental health awareness to the forefront in public education. Anxiety 
disorders are the most common mental health challenge in the U.S., impacting 54% of females and 46% of 
males, with age 7 being the average age of onset, according to the World Health Organization. While anxiety 
disorders are highly treatable, only one-third of those suffering receive treatment. Educating our teachers, parents 
and community members is paramount and plays a role in the education of the entire child. 
I have a Finance Degree along with 20 years in public accounting & tax and nonprofit accounting. I have served 
on various community boards in the Greater Grand Rapids area and have a passion for education and anything 
‘kid’ related. 
As a member of the MASB Board of Directors, I hope to continue the great work being done by the MASB 
‘to provide quality educational leadership services for all Michigan boards of education, and to advocate for 
student achievement and public education.’

every board member should find it beneficial and effective to be active in MASB.
Serving our member boards will be accomplished by providing quality and accessible training, timely and 
relevant classes as well as maintain the solid foundation that they have established with the 100-level courses.

district constituencies and encourage them to advocate for public education. In this age of information overload 
this will continue to be one of our greatest challenges.
Finally, as a board member of the MASB I will continue to support our Annual Leadership Conference with its 
unmeasurable value of networking and team building and focus on current up-to-the-minute issues, training and 
best practices. This is also our opportunity to share emerging thoughts and directions in public education that will 
spark the innovation and focus on student achievement. It would be my dream for every Michigan public school 
board member to attend the ALC and feel that it was the best use of their time and money, and that they are 
better off going, then not going. We must continue to encourage all the participants to be lifelong learners and 
then taking the excitement and knowledge back to their districts and counties and regions and making impact. 
MASB can, is and should be the catalyst in the chemical reaction to inspire, encourage and set the bar high for 
public education in Michigan. 
I am fortunate to come from a district that has benefited from all that MASB has to offer. My home district has a 
long list of items that have been brought back from conferences in Michigan and national conferences that still 
benefit us today. It would be my privilege to serve you as your board representative and pay some of this debt 
back.
